                  OA06_LIGHT, SOUND, MOVEMENT WORKSHOP (1949-51)
                  This taller will be distributed for three consecutive years in the BMC. It was first
                  organized in 1949 when the marriage initiative was initiated by Betty and Pete
                  Jennerjahn, who repeated the following year. A year later, during the verano of 1951
                  was returned by Katherine Litz and MC Richards.

                  The Light, Sound, Movement workshop will be a taller exploration of light, sound and
                  movement and its interrelation in the scenario. Experiment with artificial light through
                  the projectors, its interaction with the color and the dress, with the sound through the
                  music and the voices and with the movement using the body as music and expression
                  through the dance.

                  Los Jennerjahn in collaboration with people from the community, alumni and
                  professors: Nicholas Cernovich, Mark Hedden, Don Alter, Tim Lafarge, Dorothea
                  Rockburne, Vera Williams, June Christensen, MC Richards, Mary Fiore, Victor Kalos,
                  Jay Watt, Dan Rice and Frank Moore, improvised theatrical pieces of short duration,
                  approximately one minute, with an experimental format that allowed exploring the
                  interactions of the light, the sound and the movement. Fragments can arise from
                  technical ideas like, for example, the reaction of a little red light or of the desire to
                  project an awareness, a collection and a sensation in all possible directions.



                  The taller initially organized by the Jennerjahn, consisted of a total of documents
                  that were part of the ideas of the participants and were tired of releasing and
                  designing the light, the sound and the movement. Different fragments realized by the
                  alumni that took place in the scenario exploring distinct ideas and techniques.
                  Some of the pieces represented in the course include: Square Dance, Hexameter,
                  Toy Dog and Fly, Before Dawn, Stripes, Spot Dance, Chinese Teacup, Drawingroom
                  Dance, Pendulum, Confinement, Pacific Lament, Doors.

                  All the taller stories are realized with the available recursos, which are minimum, with
                  the self-production as the idea that involves the realization, generating unique
                  activities with the azar and the improvisation is the protagonists.
                  It is part of a group of people with concrete interests and a way of hearing

                  OA07_THE GLYPH (1951)
                   Enter the actions taken by Olson at the college, in the 1951 version of The Glyph. This representation
                   encompasses his theater curriculum and poetry which detaches the importance of spontaneity and
                  interdisciplinary collaboration. A new multimedia activity based on the roots of poetry that was inspired by
                   the Mayan glifos, on a four-country estate in the Yucatán. The double character of the glifo, like writing
                   and images, is fascinating for its geometric, plant and animal shapes and sizes in stone, in the same way
                   as the writing of a civilization.




                   The achievement consists of a unique work in turn with the common idea of glifo, expressed by the
                   different artistic forms and carried out in collaboration with Lou Harrison, Ben Shahn and Katherine Litz.
                   An image composed contained in one single part: poem-glifo, pintura-glifo, dance-glifo. Un “intercambio
                   de regalos-glifo”46 as defined by Litz, structured in its parts, a first of its participants Olson and Shahn
                  and other segments with Litz and Harrison, but also interconnected and independent to the vez.




                   In the first part of the action, there is nothing clear about the poem that Olson wrote to Shahn47, among
                   other investigators48, it was written by the Glyph poem , one verse dedicated to the marriage of Shahn
                   and Alvin, the sister of Jack Lipsey, a cocinero del BMC.

                   Shahn's drawing, A glyph for Charles, was a torso with a head like a black armadura
                   with geometric drawings. Weighing our preservation, we appear in a photograph
                   from the documentary Denise Levertov and Charles Olson49.



                  In the second part, Harrison composed A glyph for Kathy Litz, a composition of six
                  movements by piano, backs, keys, horns and, possibly, a gong. The score includes
                  notes and instructions which are free to improvise. For his part, Litz prepared a 9-
                  minute dance solo, based on Harrison's score. In his coreography, Glyph Dance,
                  appeared and revealed a biombo, with Shahn, dancing and creating distinct
                  characters. Jugaba with the movement and the dress, which consists of a large black
                  jersey that handled with its leather50. In this part, the body is intended to support
                  action and new configurations in the space.



                  In fact, it has a unique concept, the glifo, is structured into a multidisciplinary action
                  materialized in the language of expression of each of the disciplines. The glifo is
                  used as a source of artistic production, where the spontaneity appears as a category
                  in the process, in a brush with the essence of the propio concept. An action of playful
                  character, like an interchange of joys between friends, which highlights distinct forms
                  of expression and communication through the transformative capacity of the glifo:
                  “The main idea of the glifo expressed by the different artistic forms will be an
                  integrated image contained in one sola obra”51.

                   Activate the Cage proprietor, also Robert Rauschenberg, David Tudor, Merce Cunningham, Charles
                   Olson, MC Richards and alumni and dancers of the Cunnigham class like Nicholas Cernovich. A
                   collaborative work with a duration of one hour54, which was designed in the BMC room and which does
                   not exist in images or recordings, although it is really exceptional holding in cuenta that many of the
                   activities were recorded by the professors and students. This contribution also contributes to the growth
                   of the world and the importance of this first happening.




                   The origin of Theater Piece No.1 arises from a long-running conversation between Cage and Tudor in
                   the middle of the day that came to Cabo55 and shows the importance of their encounters as the source
                   of inspiration and the continual interchange of ideas at the college. From this conversation, Cage created
                   a rapid sketch of the event, with the actions that took place in the various periods of time, but still
                   liberated in the determined fragments of the improvisation and defining a space organization where the
                   people were located in the center. sillas enfrentadas.




                   The action contemplates a multiple participation in temporal segments, created by Cage, which is isolated
                   without the actions related to one with the other. Then we determined the localizations of each of the
                   actions and participants, incorporating the ambient sound into the composition and, in this way, redefining
                   the musical score to replace it with the temporal fragments56 :




                                   Projector
                                   Begins at 16min.
                                   Play freely until 23min.
                                   Begin again at 24.30min.
                                   Play freely until 35.45min Begin
                                   at 38.20min.
                                   Play freely until 44.45min.


                   The spatial arrangement is fundamental for the removal of the various parts. The public found itself in
                   chairs organized in four triangles that were placed in the center, forming a square in the center and
                  diagonals that were already empty. This provision allows the participants to film among the spectators.

                   other recordings. Tudor played the piano, possibly the composition Water Music59, Olson and Richards
                   lean their poems from the alto of scales minetras that Cunningham, accompanied by other dancers,
                   danced between the spectators. As part of the action, a dog appeared in scene persiguiendo to

                   A catalyst for this representation, recognized by Cage, is the text by Antonin Artaud, The Theater and Its
                  Double, translated by MC Richards in English, which extols the idea that “the theater can exist outside of
                   a text, (… ) that all of you can be free to be at risk (…). In this way, we do not direct the attention of the
                   public into a concrete direction.”61 One of the cruelty theaters that introduces a new concept of space
                   with distinct actions, movements and objects occurring simultaneously, generating amazement.




                  Aunque Cage establishes temporal limits for each participant, existing distinct moments without being
                   able to predict the result of the combinations that are maintained. Depending on the instructions, the
                   composition never sounds at all times, it may depend on completely random elements, such as the light
                   and the time the piece plays. There is a fragment of the narrative discourse, using methods based on
                  random parameters of I Ching and introducing the azar and the impregnable as a category in the subject,
                   combined with the effective choice of silences as there is no action change. A position that manifested
                   its renunciation of the intention in favor of the azar to compose its obras, through the I-Ching as the
                   oracle that responded to its preguntas.




                  The unique focus was removed from the viewer's experience, which was converted into the subject
                   located in the middle of the happening and with which this direct communication was established. The
                   action is based on the interaction of the agents with a spectator-actor who must move to be able to
                   appreciate the multiple attention points that emerge in the distinct directions and spaces, so that they
                   participate not only in the middle of the view, but also across the body, which must adapt to the
                   movement of the action. A pioneering multisensory experience that amplifies the scenario more to the
                   public. The reactions before the happening were generally accepting and surprising, rejected the new
                   and extra vivid ones, there were also divergent opinions like that of Stefan Wolpe, and that we did not
                   pare in absolute fascination and abandoned the spectacle62 or that of Johanna Jalowetz63.

                   This is an adaptation to be converted into a mito by the structure of your
                   action, the surprising effect and the collaboration between disciplines, which John Cage describes as
                  “involuntary… without finality, an anarchic situation (…).”64A hibridación of distinct actions and artistic
                   disciplines that converge into actions that unfold in an autonomous form without the coordination or the
                   dialogue, in a new artistic practice where the azar, the improvisation, the spontaneity, the caos and the

                   This is the same, Cage is made of one of the most famous and transverse parts of the BMC: on its part
                  4´33´´65. This is a compuesta of three movements and has a stable durability for the I-Ching employee,
                   just as in the previous example.
                   The music plays in silence, siguiendo a score in white, passing the songs of the score while they are
                   stable in each part, without extra or single note lasting for 4.33 minutes of playing time. In this
                   composition, there are practical ideas that matured during the last decade: the equipment of art and life,
                   the need to eliminate the intention and imagination of music, the awareness to appreciate everything
                   and every sound, The total rechazo intervenes in a world ordenado generated harmony and the mix of
                   sound and silence as the key to this composition.




                   Many of these characteristics can be encountered in the analysis of the happening
                   from BMC.



                  OA09_ WATER MUSIC (1952)
                   This mismo verano Cage also lleva a cabo otra action, a modo de concierto para la comunidad del
                   college. A piano musical composition composed by voice, with a duration of 6.40 minutes and without
                   any musical or narrative structure. In the Water Music there is a piano, a radio, three speakers, a few
                   water containers, a wooden blade, a mechero and a cigarro, a game with cards and a clock. The pieza,
                   composed in 1952, was released in New York on 2 May and was interpreted by the BMC as being true.
                   It is considered one of its first performed and preceding compositions from its Water Walk of 1959.




                   The score is composed of several songs that were thought to be created by a cartel. There appear 41
                  events noted in all details (piano notes, radio frequencies, movements with the sound, etc.) and with
                   the times assigned to each action in minutes, seconds and microsegundos. Cage already have some
                  notes already so that the performer performs as desired, but prescribes the beginnings and finals and
                   establishes the instructions of the various actions. The representation begins to barajando the set of
                   cards to determine the order of the actions and uses a clock to establish the time limits. An exceptional
                   musical piece for the use of everyday objects and water to explore different sounds.

                   For another part, the propia score, conceived as a set of fragments that unite to

                   to be observed, which accompany the music and the sounds, introducing ideas from

                   object.


                  OA10_ TAKE CARE OF BRUNHILDE! (1952)
                  Finally, in the verano of 1952, there were multiple representations and concerts,
                  which included the title Take Care of Brunhilde!, created by Cunningham and MC
                  Richards. A theatrical scene inspired by Der Ring, 1874, by Richard Wagner and
                  Occuppe-toi d`Amelie!, 1908, by Georges Feydeau.

                   A collective performance with the participation of Hilda Morley, Franz Kline, Nick
                   Cernovich, Charles Olson, Katherine Litz and Remy Charlip, which built a scene that
                   included a lake, live music and dance. A collage of influences with a unique
                   representation mode that reflects the intention of our creators in fomenting
                   interdisciplinary relationships. It deals with collaborations and interchanges between
                   disciplines that are the foundation of Cunningham's coreographies68, where we
                   investigate the possibilities that are limited to the body in a radical expansion in
                   space, at the same time as in technology, collaborating with various musicians and
                   artists to eject their pieces. .




                  68
                     Merce Cunningham was born in 1953 and was founded by the BMC in his dance company, Merce
                  Cunningham Dance Company, composed by dancers: Carolyn Brown, Viola Farber, Paul Taylor and
                  Remy Charlip. Among his usual collaborators we met John Cage and David Tudor, as musicians and
                  Robert Rauschenberg, as a scene from 1954-64, with whom they coincided at the college. Subsequently,
                  we collaborated, incorporated as artistic partners, with Jasper Johns between 1967-80, Mark Lancaster
                  from 1980-84 and William Anastasi and Dove Bradshaw in 1984. However, the length of the existence
                  of the company, todavía in activity, was realized. collaborations with other artists and architects like
                  Andy Warhol, Roy Lichtenstein, Richard Serra, Isamu Noguchi and Benedetta Tagliabue.

                   3.3 ARQUITECTÓNIC ACCOUNTS




                  Continuation is analyzed, in chronological order, with the following details in the BMC where the
                   architecture is presented through the agents involved or the property action. All of them emerged in a
                   period of time that began with the appearance of the college in 1933 until 1949 and is one of the most
                  relevant papers in the discipline within the academic configuration of the BMC.




                  AA01_THE FARM (1933-1956)
                   The cultivation of productive soils and the development of the granja had its origins in a propuesta in the
                   finals of 1933 with three students: Norman Weston, Anne Chapin and David Bailey. The main object was
                  to auto-abastecer the college
                   with frescoes products, with the consiguiente ahorro, but also with the idea that you want to build
                   community. This initiative, which was unanimously approved69, constitutes the origin of the Work
                   Program, which was formally formalized in 1936.




                   To design the project, Norman Weston produced a plan that was presented in March 193470, which was
                   divided into and detailed the various areas with the requirements and cultivations to plant each site. The
                   plan includes the removal of 67 hectares, approximately, which is available and an estimated estimate
                   for green and winter crops. The cultivable stone is located around the lake near the Blue Ridge Assembly,
                   at a distance of approximately one kilometer from Robert E. Hall.




                  In all classes, from 1:00 p.m. to 4:00 p.m., you will find out about different tasks in the classroom and on
                   the grounds that complete your training, and that you also collaborate with the teachers. These activities
                   are part of the maintenance work at the Blue Ridge Assembly, where Bascombe Allen participated, who
                   is responsible for the BMC installations and who is involved in the project of the farm, with our knowledge
                  and partners. with the resto de miembros de la comunidad71. This initial team also formed part of Albers
                   as responsible for the maintenance of roads, roads and marriage Lipsey, trained by Jack and Rubye, who
                   helped in the kitchen of the college. These physical works, in which students and professors compare
                   with each other and struggle, collaborate in the desaparición of jerarquías fuera de las classes72
                   reforzando la idea de comunidad.




                  During the first years of the project, in 1937, Dreier realized a reflection on the possibilities of this activity
                   that proportioned greens, meat

                  AA02_SPECTODRAMA: PLAY, LIFE, ILLUSION (1936 y 1937)
                   In the Stage Studies course led by Schawinsky, students include the functions of artists, musicians, actors,
                   writers and speakers. In organizing the Spectodrama scene representations,


                   based on concepts related to optics, shape, color, acoustics, sound, language, time, architecture, music,
                   technology and illusion.




                   The first representation, in 1936, was based on the miracle and music with improvised music by John Evarts79,
                   in which he participated, apart from Schawinsky, Allan Sly80 and students: Beverly Coleman and George
                  Hendrickson.


                   The segment took place at the end of the 1937 course and incorporated the music of Kurt Schwitters “Basic
                  Sound Sonata”. Between 1936 and 1938, Schawinsky directed three performances of Spectodrama, both at the
                   BMC and at the Cummington School for Music and Art. Previously we represented several scenes based on our
                  designs, experiments and improvisations in 1925-26 in the Experimental Theater of the Bauhaus in Dessau82,
                   employing the hereditary methodology of Oskar Schlemmer which was based on the observation of the game
                   between the heads in relation with it entorno.




                  Spectacle unfolded in the scenario, experimenting with the spatial relationships between and through the hearts.
                   A new scenario formatted by a background image and a black color cortina, with a visual format that also
                  introduces technological innovations like projection and cinema. Una obra dearrollada en four parts, previamente
                  structured, pero que se representedaban introduciendo techniques de improvisation ación. Each of the parts
                   has a determined scene and outfit, to hide or reveal the orientation and layout of the actor respecting the
                   proportion and size of the actor.

                   space. The story that continues is based on scenes represented at the Bauhaus in
                   Dessau, on courses taught at the BMC from 1936 to 1938 and, subsequently, at the
                   Cummington School for Music and Art83

                  First of all, we approach optics through scenes, including simple geometric shapes and
                  colors, accompanied by music, and by protagonists. In this case, each instrument
                  corresponds to a certain color (cello-azul, clarinete-rojo, trompeta-amarillo). Distintas
                  geometries in back dimensions and colors that transform into a function of light
                  interaction, generate a set of shadows coordinated by the music, until they disappear
                  into the scene number of one. In the final scenes, the protagonists of the visual
                  representation methods show a large eye and distinct drawings, quadrants and
                  photographs of distinct periods and styles. Salía a scene, entonces, an actor who
                  explained the function of the eye and the feeling of the view.

                  These are the reflections that relate the view to the perception of the universe, which
                  reveals the darkness through the ausencia of vision through the intervention of one of
                  the actors, with the tapadoes of the eyes, which is discussed as the colors and the
                  perception of the mismos in its particular universe.

                   The segment, focused on the acoustics, is formed by five scenes in which the perception
                   of the sound is approached through elements in the scene such as, for example, a scale,
                   which is accompanied by percussion music. A scene in different levels with platforms
                   and ramps, with heads and tuning forks in motion. When a game is established between
                   music and sound, music is intermixed live with recordings and projections of all types of
                   orchestras and bands of different procedures. We did not pass the final scenes in which
                   the actors appeared with masks and glasses of different wines, sensed a mesa
                   interactuando and realized interventions with a disconnection between the sound and
                   the words, intermixed with sounds. In the final act, an actor appears who is performing
                   in a white dress that will notify one of the actors.



                   A word that I have suffered from tone hasta generar alboroto, repeating a sequence of
                   words and showing a large ear of actors most likely to be green, losing their masks and
                   coming to light with the lost lights of intensity.



                  The third part will be resolved in five steps when approaching the concept of the time.
                  Start with three moving clocks in the scene, watch mechanism projections, a moving
                  metrology, get to a recipient, the latido rhythm of a heart and the appearance of waves
                  in one of the screens. In continuation, three actors represent the normal time, slowly and

                                        “Spectodrama is an educational method that aims at the interchange of arts and
                                        sciences, which uses the theater as a laboratory and place for experimentation. The
                                        work group is made up of representatives of all the disciplines that approach the
                                       concepts and phenomena related to different points of view, and create scenic
                                        representations that express themselves”85.

                   The Schawinsky scenario was not unique to the representation, if not to the experiment. A participatory
                   investigative laboratory that examines the space and surface area, the temporal and the transitory, the
                  movement and the phenomenon, continuing the plants initiated by the Bauhaus. It was my intention to
                  use the scenario for demonstrations in which you lived through all the media and all the stories of
                   knowledge and artistic exploration, contando con la collaboration de specialists (profesores and students
                  from different departments, etc.).86The scenario is the lugar You can plant and explore different
                   architectures that have been mutated to reveal the action, in a theoretical-practical way, where you revise
                   essential concepts in architectural training. Unapuesta por el trabajo junto other profesionales de distintas
                   disciplinas para que las ideas y el conocimiento fluyan, diluyendo las bordereras que las separan.




                   These productions can be considered the germ of which soon became known as a theater performance.
                   Unlike the improvisation that characterizes the happenings of John Cage, the representations are
                   organized with a planned narrative structure, where the scene and the roles are perfectly structured and
                   played and the spectator has a pasivo role as the receiver of the actor. Spectodrama can be considered
                   an experimental theater in which it is a method of interchange between arts and sciences, using color,
                   action, movement, light, sound, words and gestures, in the bush of new ways of expression and
                   communication.




                   During his stay in the BMC, Schawinsky also proposes the day of silence, silent day87, an intention to
                  introduce a day to the week in which all the community communicates through gestures and uses a
                   misma mask. With this proposition we ask you about the new modes of expression and communication
                   that you intend to put into practice previously, as in the concrete case of a meeting with Albers, Gropius
                   and Breuer relating to the new project of the campus of the plant that we use the speech as. poor
                   communication.

                  AA03_WORK PROGRAM(1936-1956)
                   The architectural and construction program that installs the college must be considered in the same way
                   as an implementation for the construction that takes into account these actions with the end of improving
                   and improving the conditions of habitability and functionality of the installations on the campus, without
                   fail. the training necessary to learn in Cabo. But also because of the ability to adapt the community to the
                  adversities of creativity and the work together, it means that you can follow your journey. Furthermore, it
                   represents the aspect of community most removed from the BMC by its recognition and participation of
                   all members of the community: students, professors, partners and visitors, without distinction of sex.
                   Therefore, do not forget to take into account those responsible for this project, without having to take into
                   account the experience that is available if it is different. From the fact that both Kocher, Godfrey and
                  Gregory, with the assistance of the rest of the visiting professors and architects who inspired the Work
                   Program, were decisive for their compromise and exclusive dedication.




                  If we understand the Work Program as the set of actions for maintenance, construction and management
                   of the BMC, which expands the personal experience of the student with profiles and disparous interests,
                   we can affirm that the architecture is positioned as an essential and transversal discipline in the
                   convivencia. In the previous chapter it is explained that, considering the origins of the college, the
                   activities included in the program reform the interrelationship between intellectual training and physical
                   and manual work for the training of the person. Ponían en practica los conocimientos teóricos y técnicos
                   desde la voluntariedad and inexperience en muchas de las tareas a realizar, pero con la satisfacción que
                  les producía ver cómo se materializaban los distintos preyectos emprendidos.




                   The program, as part of the architectural curriculum of the BMC, is based on the educational value and
                  individual responsibility of each person. We understand the architecture as a social activity based on the
                   reality of the moment to improve the quality and conditions of life. For this reason, this is a complementary
                   format that permits additional professional experience that, in turn, requires the use of ingenuity to solve
                  the problems before they arise. In these actions we are most interested in the materials manufactured
                  and industrialized in construction, in a busqueda of optimization and abaratamiento of the recursos in
                   combination with the principles of hygiene.




                   The Work Program is converted into an application for students from other centers who want to complete
                   their training, but also provides inspiration for other interested institutions in the field of experience and
                   practice. A long time ago, when it was in operation, we were able to construct our educational buildings,
                   apart from the work of adapting the preexistences of the campus.


                   La arquitectura fue understood how it supports the physical and intellectual ideas and principles of the
                   college.


                   Of all the projects built, possibly the most significant sea el
                  Studies Building (analyzed in the previous chapter) is as much as it is scaled by its paper as the catalyst
                   for the program which is located in a preferred location at the

                  AA04_DEBATE ARQUITECTÓNICO (VERANO 1946)
                  The reason for this debate, as architecture is the object of discussion, originates
                  from the presentation to the BMC community of the project submitted to the TAC
                  studio. In February 1946 a building of dormitories for women was built, which was
                  built within the Work Program, fundamentally for students. The cargo abarcaba, in a
                  principle, the block of the dormitorios, but, one has finalized this object, acometerían
                  a plan director for the overall desarrollo of the BMC89. This project is responsible for
                  Fletcher as representative of the studio led by Gropius in Cambridge. After a first
                  visit to meet the campus and hold a first meeting with the college, there was also a
                  first meeting for preliminary studies90.



                   The policy will be that any relevant decision by the BMC community will be consulted
                   so that, in this case, it operates in the same way. The proposal was presented and
                   the members of the college were invited to write their opinions and reflections on the
                   subject, subsequently calling a meeting with the architectural director and discussing
                   the aspect of the project91.

                  This is the beginning of a debate in the community that was initiated by a student,
                  Dan Wight, who carried out an alternative project in the next year, expanding the
                  development process for a year, with the discretion of the program and the mode of
                  use of the building92. Realize a counterpropuesta in relation to the mode of living
                  and the program that TAC offers.
                  The TAC93 project defines a building of three plants with apartments for teachers
                  and student accommodation, four double rooms and a restaurant for four people.
                  These dwellings are integrated into a work area and studio, although they are not
                  included in the apartments. On the lower floor there is a large community space, an
                  almacen, a laundry room and a large terrace.

                   Before this proposal for a major escalator in which it was combined in the area of
                   studies and dormitories, Wight prepared, together with other students, an alternative
                   proposal for minor escalators intended for their students. The project led by Wight is
                   a longitudinal building articulated in the center for a common space that separates a
                   private studio and work area

                   In the same way, it was agreed that, in the academic positions of the center of

                  of follow-up and participation to study the proposals and vote on them.

                   college and in which the propuestas will be sometidas to a collective vote, situando
                   the architecture as the material defining the activities and of convivencia.

                  AA05_MINIMUM HOUSE (1947-48)
                   This achievement is the result of the self-organization and management of a group of students that,
                   before the auspices of an architectural study in the college,
                   decide to take your cue from the project of a living, in the year 1947.


                   The team trained by Ralph Chernoff, Bernard Karp, Albert Lanier, Alex Morse, Warren Outten, Sewell
                  Sillman, Ragland Watkins and Paul Williams decided to plan and build a living space for a plant intended
                  for a pair of teachers or students that included kitchen and area of studio. Following the presentation of
                   the proposal, in October, the Board of Fellows and received its view to be taken into account, including
                   the obras, which continued until the end of 1948. For the ejecución of the misma, it decided the economic
                   portability On behalf of the Williams family they donated $1,000.




                   The 100m2 living room is built with local stone walls and a large crystal facing towards the south, although
                   the fachadas east and west are covered with corrugated aluminum chapa. The house of his residence,
                   among others, Williams and his woman, saw him in 1953 as professor at the BMC. Also lived in marriage
                  with Joseph and Mary Fiore.




                   This life, which we may consider of “low cost”, arises at the initiative of a group of students interested in
                   architecture that, before the difficulties of the college by contracting to an architect, decide to assume the
                   riesgo and llevar to cabo the project of the vivienda ellos mismos. It requires independence and an
                   autonomous way of working in which the group takes responsibility for the entire process. We will hire the
                   project because we will “build it algo”109 and we will take into account its propias strategies to ensure
                   viability and that it can be implemented. A proposition that means action as a vital activity in the BMC
                   community.




                  AA06_SUPINE DOME (1948)
                   In the year 1948, Fuller was invited as a professor at the Summer Institute to assist with the object of
                   work on geodesic studies110, the subject of which he had been investigating and who had multiple
                   models and prototypes111, built on his Dymaxion caravan . . Our structural experiments are not developed
                   exclusively in the scope of industry or engineering, but only expand in cultural and artistic contexts. Su
                   vivienda Dymaxion and other structures in relation to the habitar, habían sido expuestas años antes en el
                   MoMa, por lo que el BMC resultaba un escenario ideóneo para el desarrollo de su propuesta al confluir
                   personas con diversa training, incluyendo un grand número de artists. In its theoretical discussion we
                   refer to nature and the ability of geometric structures to define human habitat, so that the geodesic cup
                   can be understood from the design of a habitable structure.

                  The idea will be to build a collective method with the participants of these classes in a
                  geodesic cup as an example of effective construction, which will allow you to log the
                  maximum resistance using minimum gas and material. For the meeting, together with
                  the student building, a team trained by professors and students met: Si Sillman, Elaine
                  De Kooning, Roger Lovelace, Josef Albers, Albert Lanier, Paul Williams, Willie Josep,
                  Patt Passlof, Irwin Lubroth, Warren Outten and Kenneth Snelson. The material available
                  to lift the box is made of aluminum strips of 2 thicknesses (one of 5 cm), of different
                  colors and bags of Venetian Persians. It conforms to the structural skeleton with the
                  object of raising a hemispherical cup of 48 feet (14.40m) in diameter, a height of 23 feet
                  (about 7m) in an area of 1,500m2 and a weight less than 270 pounds (122.47 kg).




                  The propio Fuller carried out the mathematical calculation and decided to carry out the
                  experiment to be fully aware that there was a problem with the dimensioning of the
                  supporting material. To be able to acquire sufficient resistance it will be necessary to
                  duplicate the size of the aluminum strips, but the availability of the material will be limited
                  and you will only need to acquire the elements you use. The hemisphere could not rise
                  above the ground, but this crash turned into one of the true accounts of this, which
                  formed part of the history of the BMC. The supine dome, as named by the Fuller
                  proprietor, is a project that raises the interests of all the community as much by the
                  presence and enthusiasm of Fuller112, as by the structural solution that could be
                  translated as a construction model in the college of cara a resolver los problemas de
                  escasez de lojamientos. This collective action, participation and participation in all the
                  community, is understood as an experimental process in which the crash is a part of
                  mismo.



                  Fuller's statement: “Alcanzas el éxito when we already have equivocations”113, notes
                  the need to err to conseguir the éxito, introducing the riesgo al fracaso as an active
                  element in the design process and in the learning process.
                  A working methodology focuses on other more systematic systems, with the intention
                  of generating among the participants a keen understanding of the construction of
                  structural principles in relation to the efficiency of the material and the economy.

                  AA07_GEODESIC DOME (1949)
                  The truth is, in 1949, Fuller resigned as director of the verano courses, following the
                  crisis generated by the renunciation of Dreier and the renunciation of the Albers. He
                  joined alumnae of the Institute of Design of Chicago with whom he assigned his class.
                  The group of students, named by the Twelve Disciples or The Spheres114, was trained
                  by: Louis Caviani, Arthur Boericke, Eugene Godfrey, Mary Jo Slick Godfrey, Joseph
                  Manulik, Alan Lindsay, Jeffrey Lindsay, Ysidore Martínez, Donald Richter, Robert
                  Richter, Masato Nakagawa and Harold Young.

                  During the study during the academic course of light structures and the busqueda of
                  industrialized life solutions, we saw with the object of creating a new prototype of a
                  geodesic cup, of more controllable scale and composed of well-sized resistant tubular
                  elements. In February of this year, in the gardens of the Pentagon in Washington, I had
                  a cup with the same geometry as the previous one, using tubular elements of greater
                  resistance and reinforced with a red cable. In this case, you will see the material in the
                  BMC, showing its resistance and lightness and incorporating a protective plastic cover
                  of vinyl formed by hexagons and pentagons inflates115. The solution is the result of a
                  process that reveals a stronger structure than any of the parts that make it up. In parallel
                  form, distributed to our technical classes and other projects that can be used to construct
                  prefabricated wooden molds to manufacture fiberglass cups. Allow the high temperatures
                  and humidity levels to be fragrant, so we will produce satisfactory products.




                  After the first attempt at the previous model, this is the second model, the result of your
                  investigation is valid and it helps to consolidate your relationship with Fuller's interests
                  in the prefabrication and preparation of industrial and transportable life. So, the
                  proponent as a light structure that claims to serve as a refuge in the position of posguerra
                  and as a structure that revolutionizes military construction, with the possibility of
                  commercialization. Represents an architectural solution designed to serve as an
                  alternative habitable form, incorporating a technical appeal and culminating in the
                  structure of the EE.UU Pabellón. in the World Expo in Montreal of 1967. Asimismo fue
                  el germen del concepto de tensegridad116, que más adelante patentaría.




                   114
                      Interview with Kenneth Snelson by ME Harris on May 25, 1972. In WRA, NCSA, BMC
                  Research Project 1933-1973 IV Released Interviews, Box37.
                  115
                      Interview with Jeffrey Lindsay by ME Harris, 20th of April 1972. In WRA, NCSA, BMC
                  Research Project 1933-1973 IV Released Interviews, Box40.
                  116
                      The original term is tensegrity, translated as 'tensegrity': tensional integrity. Founded by Fuller, it is a
                  structural principle based on the use of compressed components within a red tension that continues to
                  be a self-equilibrated structure. The system is mechanically stabilized at the same time, distributing and
                  balancing the traction and compression forces, but not by the resistance of the individual components,
                  but by the way in which the voltages are distributed overall in its group.

                  It is considered that the origin of this concept originated in the BMC of its encuentro with the alumnus
                  Kenneth Snelson, sculptor that based a large part of its artistic production in this concept.

                   3.4 THE ARQUITECTURA DEL ACONTECIMIENTO. PATTERNS AND CHARACTERISTICS



                  From the idea of understanding the architecture as a concept, as activity that reflects the construction, it was
                   selected by the most representative ones through the analysis of the Sucedido a lo largo de la Existencia del
                   BMC, pudiendo haber already fuera algún otro sin ninguna intencionalidad . Through the analysis of the real
                   project and the understanding of the BMC as an educational center of liberal arts, it is possible to establish a
                   series of patterns and characteristics that need to be clarified in this architectural design.




                   For one part it comes to the college as a space for integral education that promotes an ample
                   understanding of the arts and sciences, the conditions of the natural and social world and the
                   responsibility of the city, in addition to the desarrollo del juicio crítico and independent thought. A small
                   scale learning community, this question is decisive for being able to hear participation, communication
                   and knowledge between our friends.


                   In this case, the place is the cultivation base that allows the unraveling of initiatives and the confluence
                  of ideas that unravel into their distinct acontecimientos. On the other hand, we welcome the various
                   thinkers, creatives, artists and directors who will propel their actions and the participants who will actively
                   become involved in them. A decisive confluence of ideas and personas in the BMC scenario. In this way,
                   we detected the voluntariness in proposing and developing collective actions between the professors
                   and the students. Regularly, we have available material resources, certainly limited, so that we can
                   develop our properties and exploit them to provide the maximum of our options and possibilities.




                   In this way, it manifests itself as these achievements, many of them, its result of a process of academic
                   or personal work, as it happens in Spectacle or in the geodesic cup, or it can also be heard in other
                   words as a process, as Series the case of the Work Program or the architectural debate. As an example,
                   these actions will unfold into various spatial organizations that will generate new spaces within the action
                   scenario.




                   From the studio of the 17 additions of various natural elements selected, we can identify four constants
                   that our haptic experience, the synergy, the geometric pattern and the dialog of opuestos.

                  <SYNTHESIS>


                   From the approximation of the collaborative work taken into account in the context of the BMC,
                   we encounter the concept of energy, where the action of the backs or more causes generates
                  an effect superior to the sum of the individual effects. Complementary collaborations that are
                   established between the various artistic and scientific disciplines and that we enrich with each
                   of the actions and developed processes, but also with the various profiles of docents and
                   students, with training and disparities of interest and all the backgrounds of knowledge.



                   In the BMC we discovered Cunningham, who through his coreographies, changed the
                   conception of modern dance establishing a synergistic relationship in the investigation of
                   interdisciplinar form, through collaborations with artists, architects and with the introduction of
                   graphic software120. We must bear in mind that the methodology of the work is independent
                   of the traditional parts of the dance (music, coreography, scene and lighting) to explore the
                   movement of the heart in time and space, in the creative process of the past.


                   libertad to our colaboradores, including the dancers, who trataba as soloists generate a
                   synergy between the participants and the different disciplines that form part of the coreográfica
                   piece.


                   On the other hand, Fuller, through his investigations in the busqueda of the efficiency of
                   materials, structures and energy, in his relationship with ecology, nature and education, acuñó
                   various words in his language metafórico.
                  Terms like Dymaxion, Dynamic Maximum Voltage, which we refer to have known how to obtain
                  the maximum of each material and which we used in many of our designs. We also encounter
                   the concept of synergetic121, translating the castellano as synergetic, to identify and hear the
                  methods that use nature to coordinate with the universe and, moreover, to communicate with
                   experiences in which various functions ascertain the emergence of other distinct functions y
                   unique. If we encounter the analysis of our geodesic figures based on the concept of tension,
                   we can interpret it as a combination of self-supporting compression and traction forces.

                  <GEOMETRIC PATTERN>


                                              “The new form is more than the extension of the content”122
                                              Chales Olson


                  Along with the various actions, interventions, artistic and architectural productions of the
                  BMC, we encounter the use of geometry as it is common to all of them. A study of the
                  proppedades of the figures in the plano and in the space and where, mainly, it detects
                  the quadrado as abstract form plana in the bases of our artistic creations.



                  Albers explored the form through color, lo cual, which clearly manifested in his series
                  Homenaje al Cuadrado123. However, in its development during its first years in the
                  USA, it investigated the significance of the form, and in the production generated by our
                  journeys in Mexico, which can hold an architectural reading from the structural exploration
                  of the shapes, mainly of the quadrant. Geometric investigations that, in the same way,
                  are detected in authors like Kenneth Noland or in the White Paintings of Rauschenberg.
                  Both Albers and Rauschenberg worked with the “creative power of the frame”124, to
                  approach the concepts of space and time in their own right.

                  necessities and unloaded actions, many of them of spontaneous character, that turn into tests and the
                   support of the accontecido in the BMC.




                  <DIALOGUE BETWEEN OPINIONS>


                   Another pattern encountered through the various actions studied is the dialogue between opuestos that
                   is stable and can be identified in the propios of creative processes, generally between peace and silence,
                   between movement and dynamism. Marcados changes of intensity that provide rhythm to the action and
                   representations raised in the country. It is detected especially in the performing and multimedia activities
                   carried out by Schawinsky, Cage or Jennerjahn, where actions with diverse energy take place in the
                   narrative. However, we also read the coreographic pieces of Cunnigham and Katherine Litz, which carried
                  out an exploration of the space through movements and rhythmic sequences experimented by the body.




                   This idea can also be translated to the natural architectural findings, where the process can be understood
                   from the relationship between the project and the constructed reality, between the dimensions of the
                   design and the three-dimensionality of the built space or from the relationship that is established between
                   lo inmaterial and lo tangible. Confrontations that form part of the process, as in the debate generated by
                   the TAC dormitory project that sheltered discrepant positions in the seno of the community.




                   In the pictorial work of artists like Albers, Jack Tworkov, Kenneth Noland or Cy Twombly, we find this
                  relationship of contrast understood from the two-dimensionality of the link and interpreted from the
                   analysis of the form and the background. In the ambito of the escultura one can understand the
                  relationship that is

                   In this chapter we approach, firstly, the seeds and connections that can be established between

                  4.1 GERMEN AND CONNECTIONS OF BMC



                   In the first part of the work, I identified the ideological leaders, who supported the rules and allowed
                   us to establish very recent brands for our personalities. Elsewhere: John Andrew Rice, in his 30'
                   years, and then Theodore Dreier and Josef Albers, in his 40's and, finally, Charles Olson, in his
                  50' years. All those who were decisive in the rumbo and the directors of the college had their
                   disappearance in 1957, but from a global perspective, the march of Josef Albers, in 1949, supone
                   a great point of inflection in the influences sober that sustained the BMC. Albers represented the
                   European heritage of the Bauhaus and was a catalyst for European artists and professionals who
                   joined the institution. When he left for the University of Yale, the influence of the Bauhaus
                   practically faded away, appearing in other methods that allowed new connections to be established
                   with the college. Se trataba, in all cases, of experimental methodologies, in the feeling of being
                   founded in the experiment and in the busqueda of new results, no previstos of antemano. This
                   change is associated with political transformations in the USA. The initial idealism of the BMC,
                  which aims to promote social changes and rebuild democracy, will disappear before appearing to
                   be pesimismo over the present and the future that will be accompanied by conservative policies
                   and the persecution of the community of Senator Mc. Carthy.




                   The exilio forzoso de figures de la Bauhaus a EE.UU. Introducing new ideas in different universities
                   and institutions where they will be incorporated as educational materials. Considering the diffusion
                   of the New Bauhaus in the Institute of Design, which is the result of the IIT of Chicago, as the
                   great successor of the Bauhaus in the USA, we find the antecedent of the BMC as the main school
                   influenced by its theories. If the center was founded by László Moholy-Nagy, professor at the
                  Bauhaus from 1923-1928, sheltered in our doors in 1937, four years before the birth of the BMC,
                   with the marriage Albers received his marriage from Germany. Josef Albers became the first
                   maestro of the Bauhaus when he came to the USA in 1933 and, later, he was joined by Gropius,
                   Mies Van der Rohe and Moholy-Nagy.

                  Your preliminary course claims to modify the implementation of artistic education in relation to the

                                                                                                         , distinct from the
                   European center. Although the Bauhaus was an artistic center that produced artists, the profile of the BMC

                   The preliminary course, Vorkurs, originally organized by Johannes Itten in the Bauhaus, was maintained
                   at the length of the center tray, and some of its funds were modified by the rest of the instructors
                  responsible for the course. In its origins we approach the detailed study of nature through the representation
                   of materials and experiments with real materials, plastic studies of composition with materials and the
                   analysis of ancient masters .


                   El Vorkurs has a triple objectivo9 : first of all, he aspired to liberate the creative force of the student to
                   discover his artistic talent and realize his genuine creations. Secondly, I wanted to facilitate the election of
                  students through work with different materials and textures, allowing them to discover their interests and
                   serve as inspiration. Finally, present the principles of creative composition like the lines of form and color.

                  Following Itten's march in 1923, Moholy-Nagy took responsibility for the course, prompting Albers to
                   collaborate on it. The first adaptation of the program was of interest to “desperate students from the
                   awareness of three-dimensional, cultural and tensional relationships”10. After his resignation in 1928,
                   Albers returned to his course of investigation into the experience and understanding of the properties of
                   materials. A direct experiment which also takes place


                   study the phenomena of optical illusions11. The superficial appearance of the materials will be
                   investigated in its visual and tactile aspects, to be classified according to its structure, texture and fact. A
                   manipulation of the student's personal exploration, which continued in the BMC, busied itself with the
                   economy of resources, materials and work, to reveal its visual and structural possibilities. A constructive
                   thought that respects the naturalness of the material. Thought that, subsequently, it would be necessary
                  to implement it at the college.




                  Just like Albers, Schawinsky is another clear guide to the Bauhaus that introduced his method to the
                   BMC. Schawinsky, an early alumnus of Albers and Gropius, actively participated in Oskar Schlemmer's
                   theater classes and moved to the direction of the Bauhaus under Hannes Meyer, in 1928, contracted as
                   a theater professor. It is in the Bauhaus that we began to explore the concept of Spectacle Drama, where
                   the distinct aspects of the theater independently represented the idea of the total theater. An experimental
                   theater that was revived and materialized in the college with Spectacle and Dance of Macabre. With the
                   influence of Schlemmer, the scenario is transformed into a laboratory of experimentation with one
                   another, just as his master, the space, the volumes, the lights and the colors interact with the heads of
                   the actors in their parts. It deals with a methodology for translating the theoretical-practical class into the
                   scenario, where you can experiment in groups and without limits.




                   We affirm that, essentially, exist parallelisms between ambos centers through common figures that
                  compare to their creative methodologies. We are encouraged by pragmatism and learning by doing
                   lessons in practice by Dewey, present at the college. A pragmatic approach to manual work and effort in
                   relating life and art. The integration of art and craftsmanship in the college is not clear as in the Bauhaus,
                  because the curriculum of the BMC is built according to the profile of the professionals that is united with
                  the project. Without exception, from the perspective of the Work Program we can establish an analogy
                   between art and offices involved in construction.




                   The Bauhaus was a school of art, craftsmanship, design and architecture. The BMC, for its part, can be
                   considered as a university center mainly vinculado with the liberal arts and its life and confluential study.
                  Without embargo, it is easy to meet between all of our points in common. Gropius, in the conceptual
                  diagram that was developed, in 1922, based on the doctrine of the Bauhaus, located in the center of
                   architecture and construction. In certain ways, the BMC consigue ese objectivo, although during a stage,
                   through the Work Program, aunque fuese fruto de ses propias necesidades de autosuficiencia.

                  4.2 OCCUPATIONAL INFLUENCE BY THE DIÁSPORA OF SUS PROFESORES AND
                  ALUMNOS SEGUIDORES DE SU METODOLOGÍA



                                                                                 “Whatever you have to say, leave
                                                                                 the roots on, let them
                                                                                 dangle
                                                                                 And the dirt
                                                                                             just to make clear
                                                                                              where they come from” 28.
                                                                                  Charles Olson



                  In 1954, aware of the terrible economic situation that he faced in the last years,29 Olson
                  proposed a “future plan” for the college, assuming that when the doors closed, the
                  project could be refunded. A theoretical abstract diagram that locates the BMC in the
                  center and where it is located, between trazados concéntricos atravesados par dos
                  principals, proposes an idea of conceptual, programmatic and geographic atomization.




                   The new propuesta is cemented, a tenor of which can be interpreted in the esquema (figura 4.13), with four
                   main concepts (“arte”, “escritura”, “teatro” and “instituto”) which crystallize in four types of institution (an
                   editorial, a magazine, a theater and an academy) has been implemented in several satellite locations:
                   Cambridge, New York, Berkeley, Filadelfia and San Francisco.




                  The atomization that Olson projected was transformed into a diaspora of people who
                  participated in new artistic trends launched in the USA.
                  During the last years of its direction, literature and poetry emerged as the central part of
                  the educational program. Apostaba by the power to transform poetry into art, as an
                  alterator of society and politics. A speech that began to be elaborated in the BMC with
                  his poem The Kingfishers, tratado in the previous chapter, and which was not
                  promulgated. In Olson's speech: “Compañeros: Si no saben que la poética es política,
                  que los poetas son los actuales líderes políticos, los únicos que existenten, ne deberían
                  de haber venido.”30 Tras el cierre del college camenzó a trabajar en el public sector,
                  specifically in the Office of War Information, created to select and design the information
                  that is available to the North American public. The office promotes the values of freedom
                  and justice, in addition to producing a series of fiction that reflects the importance of the
                  American mission in the war31. A propagandistic work that collaborates with intellectual
                  and intellectual writers and that abandons itself to focus on writing.

                  Black Mountain Review, edited by the poet Robert Creely. In this number we invited
                  Allen Ginsberg to be an editor and we will include texts of significant figures of the
                  movement like Jack Kerouac or William Burroughs33.



                   This connection means that the ideas of the BMC are translated to New York,
                   converted into an artistic and cultural focus where these writings converge with
                   artists of new currents. The city has a large number of students, artists of abstract
                   expression and movement of beats, who meet in the Cedar Bar, in the Fugazi or in
                   the San Remo34, located in Greenwich Village.

                  In this environment of the new city you also see happenings,
                  as a medium of artistic expression liberated from the traditional medium, with an
                  unpredictable result and that no dialogue or coordination between its parts is allowed.
                  The artist Alan Kaprow, follower of Dewey and former alumnus of Cage at the New
                  School for Social Research, returned to his lectures. This object will convert existence
                  into an aesthetic experience. Query that it will be provided with aesthetic attention to
                  the daily life, tal and as Cage has been made with the noises. Con ello se estaba
                  realizando “a moral act, a toma of human position of maximum urgency, whereby
                  prima no es el status profesional como arte, sino la certeza de su compromiseo
                  existential”35. Below the title Eighteen happenings in six parts took place, in 1959 at
                  the Reuben Gallery, its first public happening . A type of collage of actions in very
                  transparent cubes with light, sound, projections and with the influence of the azar,
                  basis of the work of his master. These three pieces are located in different locations
                  of the gallery and in the action participated by the Kaprow proprietor with his clients.
                  The spectators were divided randomly into groups and transferred from one place to
                  another in the gallery36.
                  The happening, with one set and five guests, between those who met Cage, you did
                  not expect to be surprised and you were critical of the repetition of the movements.
                  Según Cage, Kaprow has abused the public obliged to move, with the intention of
                  focusing its attention on certain actions and considering that the acontecido is the
                  right to interpret as an artistic approximation to the common life37. However, this
                  event is the result of similar manifestations in the new artistic scene with creators
                  like Claes Oldenburg, Robert Whitman, Jim Dine or Red Grooms.

                   This idea of converting life into art is created by other artists like Jurgis Maÿiÿnas, architect and artist
                   founder of the community Fluxus, formed by artists, architects, composers and designers of various
                   origins, like Joseph Beuys, Nam June Paik or Yoko Ono. Fluxus generaba propuestas radicals en las
                   que se introducían el juego y la ironía. Tal y como se promulgaba en su manifiesto de 1963, un
                   miembro de Fluxus debía, en primer lugar,'purgar' el mundo de la “enfermedadad burguesa”, de la
                   “culture intellectual, professional y commercializada”, del “arte muerto, la imitation, el arte artificial, el
                   arte abstracto, el arte illusionista, el arte en serie”. In the second place, we must 'promote' the
                   revolution in the arts so that it can be understood by all the world. Finally, by 'fusion', “unify the areas
                   of the cultural, social and political revolution in a unified time and in action”38.




                   The idea of breaking the distance between the public and the actors of the happening was taken up
                   by experimental theater colectivos that were incorporated into the spectators in addition to the shows.
                   Among these theater groups is The Living Theater, from New York, founded by Julian Beck and
                  Judith Malina. We collaborated, among others, with various BMC participants like John Cage, Merce
                   Cunningham, Willem de Kooning, MC Richards or Paul Goodman.



                   The happening as an artistic manifestation was appropriated by various artists, but also by activists
                   like Abbie Hoffman. We consider that this is the employee to demonstrate disconformity with the
                   Vietnam War, intending to levitarize the Pentagon using the psychological energy of the group of
                   demonstrators or in their action to the attacks of the Bolsa de Nueva York, launching false tickets as
                   their muestra anti-capitalist posture.



                   In the line of experimentation of the happening we include the Merry Pranksters, a community created
                   in California in 1964 by the writer Ken Kesey, addicted to the drug LSD, acidic acid, and capable of
                   sheltering the mind and releasing the constrictions of the style of American life. A vital, aesthetic and
                   playful experiment, which took a bus from California to New York, recorded interior and exterior
                   sounds and filmed the audio. One of our events more aware of La Prueba del Ácido39, a multi-
                  tudinary fiesta where it combines various artistic manifestations and the massive volume of aluminum.



                   In it, images, films and recorded sounds are projected on tour, accompanied by live music and a light
                   experiment, along with the generation of a musical atmosphere. All you have to do is remove a
                   geodesic cup that makes it easier to settle down and enjoy what all the action is. In one way, Kesey
                  transformed into an algo festival and happening, following groups like The Diggers, the San Francisco
                   Mime Troupe or the Youth International Party, known as the Yippies.




                   The community aspect of the college continues with some of our friends like Paul Williams, who
                   created the cooperative community, Gate Hill, in Stony
                   Point, New York, in 1954, where we met with some of our companions

                   BMC like John Cage, MC Richards and Karen and David Weinrib40. Also the

                   Sigma, intended to found, in 1964, a type of self-governing university to revolutionize

                  In the educational aspect, the BMC was a precursor of another type of center in
                  North America, which arose influenced by some of its propuestas, as it was cited in
                  chapter 1 of the present work. Included in 1980, the College of Arts, established at
                  the State University of New York, in Buffalo, was named Black Mountain College II,
                  The College of the Creative Arts41. Likewise, some students who passed through
                  the BMC dedicated part of their professional life to education, while also being
                  influenced by their passage through the college. In this regard, we meet the scholar
                  Ruth Asawa, alumna of Albers and Fuller, who founded the Alvarado School Arts
                  Workshop, a program for the inclusion of art in public schools in San Francisco that
                  involved professional artists and the fathers of the alumni. Unapuesta por el arte
                  donde se bajo la opportunity de trabajar con artists profesionales para despertar la
                  largo del studenta y mejorar su dearrollo individual, aspecto amply defendido por
                  Asawa a lo largo de su traductoria42. This program participated, among others,
                  Fuller.

                   For another part, many of the college professionals will take part as docentes. Merce
                   Cunningham created his dance company in the BMC, but in a more expansive way
                   he created a school based on his methodology and exploration of the movement in
                   the margins of music, revolutionizing contemporary dance. His legacy lives on
                   through the Merce Cunningham Trust, which manages his artistic legacy and his
                   dance company, organizing representations and stories which are distributed by his
                   technique. Another clear example is represented by Josef Albers, who continued his
                   educational work at the University of Yale and that, in his publication Interaction of
                   Color of 1963, recoge part of his investigation into the perception of color and the
                   discrepancy between the physical body and visual perception.



                  In the field of architecture, the repercusión of figures like Fuller transcended the
                  temporal limits of the BMC, bringing real and utopian solutions that influenced
                  architects and investigators. A sample of our famous geodesic cups, which are
                  modeled on the town of Drop City43, of Colorado, in the middle of our last years. O
                  el Movie Drome, designed by BMC alumni and cinematographer, Stan Vanderbeek,
                  to project films. These ideas were reflected in the organization Experiments in Art
                  and Technology, EAT, founded in 1967 by artists Robert Rauschenberg and Robert
                  Whitman, together with engineers Billy Klüver and Fred Waldhau. An organization
                  centered on the collaboration between artists and engineers to develop joint projects
                  exploring the possibilities of art and technology.
